# Consideration of funding proposals – Addendum VIII

## Funding proposal package for FP266

### Summary

The addendum contains the following seven parts:

1. A funding proposal titled "Strengthening the resilience of ecosystems and communities to climate change impacts in the Pacific region"
2. Non-disclosure letter issued by the national-designated authority(ies) or focal point(s)
3. Environmental and social report(s), disclosure
4. Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment;
5. Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment summary
6. Register form for an accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment process
7. Gender documentation

Please note that this addendum is part of the funding proposal package for FP266 and should be read in conjunction with the main proposal document.

## Rule 16 Accredited Entities in the use of the funding proposal template

- Accredited Entities should provide summary information in the proposal with clear references to the detailed information contained in the annexes.
- Accredited Entities should ensure that proposals are consistent with the results provided by the GCF Accreditation Unit and the relevant guidance documents.
- Accredited Entities should manage the funding proposal including general staff not exceed 60 person-months. Including the proposal team will not be assessed with the usual service standard.
- Accredited Entities should use the [GCF Accredited Entity Funding Proposal Template](https://www.greenclimate.fund) for developing and submitting proposals to ensure consistency, clarity, and completeness of information.
- Accredited Entities are encouraged to seek support from the GCF Secretariat if needed.

Please submit the completed proposal to [proposals@greenclimate.fund](mailto:proposals@greenclimate.fund).

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.2: PAGE 3

## PROJECT PROGRAMME INFORMATION

### B.1 Climate context (more than 1000 words, approximately 2 pages)

**Context**

Climate change is a reality, globally recognised. Multiple studies have shown that the earth's climate has warmed by about 1°C since pre-industrial times. This warming is primarily due to increased greenhouse gas emissions from human activities such as burning fossil fuels and deforestation. The Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) reports indicate that without significant reductions in greenhouse gas emissions, global temperatures are likely to rise by 2-3°C or more by the end of this century.

**Impacts**

The impacts of climate change vary widely across regions and sectors. In Africa, climate change is expected to have severe consequences for agriculture, water resources, human health, and ecosystems. Rising temperatures and changes in precipitation patterns will affect crop yields, livestock productivity, and water availability. Increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events such as droughts, floods, and storms will exacerbate existing vulnerabilities.

**Historical Data**

Observations of historical climate trends show that the mean annual temperature in Mufulira has increased by 0.7°C over the past century. This warming trend is consistent with global observations and has been projected for the Southern African Development Community (SADC) region. The frequency and intensity of extreme weather events have also increased, particularly in the last two decades.

**Future Projections**

Climate models project that temperatures will continue to rise unless significant reductions in greenhouse gas emissions are achieved. By 2100, global warming is projected to reach between 1.5°C and 4.9°C relative to pre-industrial levels, depending on future emission scenarios. In Africa, climate change impacts include increased frequency of droughts, floods, and heatwaves, which will affect agriculture, water resources, and human health.

**Adaptation Measures**

To mitigate the effects of climate change, various adaptation measures are being implemented. These include improving agricultural practices to enhance crop resilience, developing early warning systems for extreme weather events, and investing in renewable energy sources. Additionally, efforts are underway to improve water management strategies and implement climate-smart urban planning.

### B.2 Project Summary

**Project Title**

Green Climate Fund Proposal

**Objective**

The objective of this proposal is to outline the project's goals, activities, and expected outcomes. The project aims to enhance climate resilience in vulnerable communities by implementing sustainable practices and technologies.

**Project Duration**

The project will span a period of five years, starting from the approval date to deliver tangible results within a reasonable timeframe.

**Funding Requirements**

An amount of $500,000 is required to fund the initial phase of the project. This funding will be used to cover the costs associated with planning, implementation, and monitoring activities.

**Partnerships**

The project will involve partnerships with local governments, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and community groups to ensure effective implementation and sustainability.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.2 PAGE 4

## B

Observed potential temperature (EPT) values are high at all four sites (1390-2680 m asl), with the highest values occurring in the uppermost part of the soil profile, indicating that water loss via ETP results in larger water loss via precipitation.

Sand content is driven by the presence of sandy soils in the court of Maribela valley. 5% more sand content was observed at the site compared to the other sites. The soil texture is sandy loam with very low permeability and high infiltration capacity.

All four target sites are projected to experience an increase in both minimum and maximum temperatures under future RCP 4.5 (moderate) and RCP 8.5 (high emission) scenarios, with a significant increase in the number of days above 30°C. The highest increase is observed at the RCP 8.5 scenario for the period 2071-2100.

The expected temperature change under future climate scenarios shows that for current temperatures, an increase by 2020 is projected (RCP 4.5 and RCP 8.5). The expected precipitation change is also significant with a decrease of up to 30% in the period 2071-2100.

EPT is projected to increase across all four sites by 2020 under both scenarios (RCP 4.5 and RCP 8.5). The highest EPT values are expected at the site with the highest elevation (site 3) and the lowest EPT values are expected at the site with the lowest elevation (site 1).

CO2 emissions are projected to increase by up to 60% under both scenarios, with a significant increase in the number of days above 30°C. The highest CO2 emissions are expected at the site with the highest elevation (site 3) and the lowest CO2 emissions are expected at the site with the lowest elevation (site 1).

Although this region is experiencing drought conditions in most regions across the county, the local government has initiated a local project to improve water use efficiency. The project aims to increase water use efficiency by up to 50% through improved irrigation practices and water conservation measures.

### B.2 (A) Theory of change narrative and diagram (max. 1500 words, approximately 3 pages plus diagrams)

#### Activity 1

**Activity Title:** Water Conservation Project in Maribela Valley

**Objective:** To improve water use efficiency by up to 50% through improved irrigation practices.

**Description:**

- Conduct a baseline assessment of current water use and identify areas for improvement.
- Develop and implement a water conservation plan that includes:
  - Improved irrigation techniques
  - Water-efficient crops and varieties
  - Rainwater harvesting systems

#### Activity 2

**Activity Title:** Community Engagement and Education

**Objective:** To raise awareness about the importance of water conservation among local farmers.

**Description:**

- Organize community meetings to educate farmers on best practices for water use.
- Develop educational materials (brochures, posters) in local languages.
- Conduct training sessions on water-efficient farming techniques.

#### Activity 3

**Activity Title:** Monitoring and Evaluation

**Objective:** To track progress towards the project's goals and ensure sustainability.

**Description:**

- Establish a monitoring system to track water use efficiency improvements.
- Collect data on water savings, crop yields, and farmer satisfaction.
- Conduct regular evaluations to identify areas for improvement and adjust strategies as needed.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.2: PAGE 1

## Problem statement

Climate change is a complex problem that demands a holistic approach to address its multiple dimensions. Addressing the root causes of climate change requires the combined efforts and sustained commitment of all sectors of society. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is designed to support developing countries in their efforts to respond to the challenges posed by climate change, including mitigation and adaptation.

### Key objectives

1. **Mitigation**: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions and promote the development and transfer of technology.
2. **Adaptation**: Enhance adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience, and reduce vulnerability.
3. **Capacity Building**: Support developing countries in building their capacities to manage climate change impacts.

### Figure 1 (not shown here)

Without urgent intervention, past processes will worsen in the future. The Safaris Desert is expected to spread both northwards and southwards, affecting the livelihoods of local communities. The main objective is to use the GCF to support sustainable land management practices that improve water security and reduce carbon emissions.

### Potential additional outcomes

An approach that integrates the long-term goals as the problem evolves opens up opportunities for expanding the impacts of the project. This includes the potential to provide employment, goods, and services to create economic value while conserving natural resources. The project will also contribute to the development of local human institutions and 12% of productive energy to facilitate the mobilization and management of local communities.

### Benefits to local communities

The project will benefit local communities by providing them with access to sustainable livelihoods, employment opportunities, and improved water security. By providing an alternative source of income, it current enhances-based livelihoods and reduces pressure on natural resources. The project also contributes to inclusive community development.

### Barriers to the potential solution

The main barriers to implementing adaptation interventions at the community level include limited access to finance, as well as government and non-governmental organizations in Marrakech. New, limited access to finance is a major barrier to implementing climate change mitigation projects. The lack of capacity to implement these projects is also a significant challenge.

### Barrier 1: Limited technical capacity

The lack of technical capacity limits the ability to implement and sustain effective management of the community-level interventions. This includes the need for technical expertise, financial resources, and institutional support. The lack of technical capacity can lead to poor project design, implementation, and monitoring.

### Barrier 2: Lack of political will

Political will is essential for the successful implementation of climate change mitigation projects. Without strong political support, it is difficult to secure funding and implement effective interventions. Political will is also necessary to ensure that climate change mitigation projects are integrated into national development plans and policies.

### Barrier 3: Limited financial resources

Limited financial resources can hinder the ability to implement and sustain effective management of community-level interventions. This includes the need for technical expertise, financial resources, and institutional support. The lack of financial resources can lead to poor project design, implementation, and monitoring.

## B

### Region: Limited knowledge of climate change impacts, risks, vulnerabilities and adaptation options exists among local communities.

Communities must have access to practical knowledge and tools that enable them to understand the risks they face from climate change and to adapt to these risks. This is particularly important in areas where climate change impacts are already being felt. Communities need to be able to identify their own needs and priorities, and to develop locally appropriate adaptation strategies. This will require a range of support, including:

- **Training**: Communities must have access to training on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Information**: Communities must have access to information on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Resources**: Communities must have access to resources that enable them to implement adaptation measures.

### Better: Limited knowledge of climate change impacts, risks, vulnerabilities and adaptation options exists among local communities.

Communities must have access to practical knowledge and tools that enable them to understand the risks they face from climate change and to adapt to these risks. This is particularly important in areas where climate change impacts are already being felt. Communities need to be able to identify their own needs and priorities, and to develop locally appropriate adaptation strategies. This will require a range of support, including:

- **Training**: Communities must have access to training on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Information**: Communities must have access to information on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Resources**: Communities must have access to resources that enable them to implement adaptation measures.

### Project strategy for overcoming barriers to adaptation and achieving a paradigm shift in Montserrat

The project will support the development of a comprehensive approach to climate change adaptation, which includes:

- **Training**: Training on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Information**: Information on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Resources**: Resources that enable communities to implement adaptation measures.

### Objectives

**Objective 1**: To address all four of the sustainable development goals (SDGs) identified by establishing coordination platforms to improve communication, collaboration and cooperation among stakeholders at the local level. This will include:

- **Training**: Training on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Information**: Information on climate change impacts and adaptation options.
- **Resources**: Resources that enable communities to implement adaptation measures.

**Objective 2**: To facilitate the adoption of climate smart agriculture practices and sustainable disaster risk reduction (DRR) strategies among farmers, fishers and other vulnerable groups. This will include:

- **Training**: Training on climate smart agriculture practices and DRR strategies.
- **Information**: Information on climate smart agriculture practices and DRR strategies.
- **Resources**: Resources that enable communities to implement adaptation measures.

**Objective 3**: To enhance the resilience of water resources, ecosystems and biodiversity in Montserrat. This will include:

- **Training**: Training on water resource management and ecosystem restoration.
- **Information**: Information on water resource management and ecosystem restoration.
- **Resources**: Resources that enable communities to implement adaptation measures.

### Notes

For more information, please visit the [Green Climate Fund](https://www.greenclimate.fund) website or contact us at [info@greenclimate.fund](mailto:info@greenclimate.fund).

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.2 PAGE 7

## Purpose
and funding hungry governments to rapidly accelerate climate mitigation, climate adaptation, and ecosystem services enhancement. This will include reducing greenhouse gas emissions, enhancing carbon sinks, and promoting sustainable practices. The theory of change (ToC) is based on an ecosystem-based education (EBA) instrument that is used to restore degraded ecosystems and promote sustainable land use. The project will be supported by financial incentives, capacity building, knowledge management, and impactful public-private partnerships. This will include seed investment, waste recovery, renewable energy, and value-added agriculture productivity. We will also support the development of local green economies through microfinance, small grants, and sustainable business models. We have strengthened our capacity to deliver on these goals by engaging with local communities, promoting sustainable management, and capital building. As a solution provider, we are committed to enhancing the resilience of ecosystems and supporting the development of green economies.

## The assumptions underlying the success of the proposed project are:
1. Target communities and the government will be engaged in the implementation process.
2. Government policies will be aligned with our objectives.
3. Partnerships will be established to support long-term sustainability.
4. Businesses will provide access to various elements of sustainable agriculture practices.

## B.2 Outcomes

### Outcome 1

The solution AMJA1, AMJA2, AMMA1 and AMMA2 is based on the need and feasible implementation of the project solutions and adapted to enhancing community and ecosystem resilience in the target areas. The project will strengthen the capacity of local communities to adapt to climate change through various activities carried out by different stakeholders and enhance their livelihoods.

- **AMJA1**: Outcome 1, the project will strengthen governance and technical capacity for climate adaptation by developing and implementing climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **AMJA2**: Outcome 1, the project will strengthen governance and technical capacity for climate adaptation by developing and implementing climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **AMMA1**: Outcome 1, the project will strengthen governance and technical capacity for climate adaptation by developing and implementing climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **AMMA2**: Outcome 1, the project will strengthen governance and technical capacity for climate adaptation by developing and implementing climate-resilient infrastructure.

### Outcome 2

Outcome 2 of Outcome 3 is a project that aims to enhance the resilience of ecosystem services in relation to adaptation and land use management through an ex-situ conservation and green-grey infrastructure. Accordingly, AMJA3 will aim at enhancing community resilience by developing and implementing on-site conservation measures and promoting sustainable land use practices. Additionally, AMMA3 will aim at enhancing community resilience by developing and implementing on-site conservation measures and promoting sustainable land use practices.

- **AMJA3**: Outcome 2, the project will strengthen local governance.
- **AMMA3**: Outcome 2, the project will strengthen local governance.

### Outcome 3

Outcome 3 of Outcome 4 is a project that aims to enhance the resilience of ecosystem services in relation to adaptation and land use management through an ex-situ conservation and green-grey infrastructure. Accordingly, AMJA4 will aim at enhancing community resilience by developing and implementing on-site conservation measures and promoting sustainable land use practices. ## 8.1 Project/programme description (max. 2000 words, representing 5 pages)

The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by promoting energy efficiency and renewable energy in the tourism sector. The project will focus on small- and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) within the tourism industry, including hotels, resorts, and tour operators. The project will provide technical assistance, capacity building, and financing to support these SMEs in adopting clean technologies.

### Objective 1: Strengthened institutional capacity and access to finance for enhanced climate action

Objective 1 aims to build an enabling environment by strengthening the capacity of national institutions to implement climate change policies and programs. This includes providing training and technical assistance to government agencies, private sector entities, and civil society organizations. The project will also support the development of financial mechanisms to facilitate access to affordable financing for climate action.

### Objective 2: Improved energy efficiency in tourism facilities

Objective 2 aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by improving energy efficiency in tourism facilities. This includes providing technical assistance and capacity building to SMEs in the tourism sector, as well as promoting the adoption of renewable energy technologies. The project will also support the development of policies and regulations that encourage energy efficiency and renewable energy use.

### Objective 3: Knowledge sharing and networking

Objective 3 aims to promote knowledge sharing and networking among stakeholders in the tourism industry. This includes organizing workshops, conferences, and other events to facilitate the exchange of best practices and lessons learned. The project will also support the development of a website and online platform to provide access to resources and information on climate action in the tourism sector.

## Output 1.1: Governance structures are strengthened to support the implementation of EA measures and the achievement of EA objectives.

### Objective 1.1.1: Establish a robust governance structure for the management, sustainability and use of funding and the implementation of EA activities at regional and local levels.

Under Activity 1.1.1, the proposed GCF project will establish **CTCs** across the four target countries:
- Haiti
- Honduras
- Nicaragua
- Guatemala

These CTCs will be established for coordination and knowledge-sharing among local and regional stakeholders within the target countries. The CTCs will also serve as a platform to engage with other relevant actors, including the private sector.

To maximize the impact of these efforts, activities will focus on the following:
- **Target countries**: Haiti, Honduras, Nicaragua, Guatemala
- **Regional coordination**: Establish CTCs in each target country to oversee the following:
  - **National coordination**: CTCs will be established at the national level.
  - **Local coordination**: CTCs will also engage with local stakeholders.

### Table 1: The Project

| Output | Budget | Target Countries |
|--------|--------|------------------|
| Output 1.1.1 | $X,XXX | Haiti, Honduras, Nicaragua, Guatemala |

### Representation of the CTCs in coordination with DREDD directors in each target country to oversee the following:

- **National coordination**: CTCs will be established at the national level.
- **Local coordination**: CTCs will also engage with local stakeholders.

### Implementation

- **Governance structure**: Establish a governance structure for the management and sustainability of funding and implementation of EA activities.
- **Coordination and communication**: Ensure effective coordination and communication between DREDD directors, CTCs, and other relevant actors.
- **Capacity building**: Provide training and capacity-building support to CTC members.

### In accordance with national laws, the 12 CTCs will be formally established via appointment; however, over the course of the project, they may evolve into more formalized structures.

Each CTC will be chaired by the mayor of the respective community and its members will include:
- **Local government**: Representatives from local government.
- **Community leaders**: Local community leaders.
- **Private sector**: Representatives from the private sector.
- **Civil society organizations (CSOs)**: Representatives from CSOs.

### Early CTCs

Early CTCs will be established by the mayor of the respective community and its members will include:
- **Local government**: Representatives from local government.
- **Community leaders**: Local community leaders.
- **Private sector**: Representatives from the private sector.
- **Civil society organizations (CSOs)**: Representatives from CSOs.

These early CTCs will serve as a model for other communities to establish their own CTCs. They will also provide input into the development of national and regional coordination mechanisms, including the establishment of a national CTC.

The T20 major economies will register grants 500 parts in year over the total 3 years of the proposed project. The project is designed to be implemented in phases, with each phase building on the success of the previous activities.

Other activities include:
- Requests should be registered entries, such as MDG, CSD, conventions or treaties.
- The proposal focuses on the financial support that should support households or communities to adapt to climate change and reduce vulnerability. This could include the adoption or strengthening of climate-resilient livelihoods, or EIA and SEA processes that ensure climate resilience is considered in development planning.
- The project will be implemented through a combination of training, capacity building, and technical assistance.
- Level of co-financing contribution should be 50% of the total cost of the project. This could include contributions from other sources such as grants or loans.
- The project will be implemented over 3 years with an initial budget of $20,000.

## B.4 Implementation arrangements (approx 1000 words, approximately 3 pages plus diagrams)

### Government structures

The implementation arrangements and governance structure for the proposed project are illustrated in Figure B.4.

![Figure B.4](image_url)

#### Key Elements:

- **Governance Structure**: Outlines the hierarchical arrangement of decision-making bodies.
- **Roles and Responsibilities**: Defines specific roles and responsibilities within the governance framework.
- **Communication Channels**: Specifies how information will flow between different levels of governance.

### Accreditation Entry

To ensure that every entity that is eligible to be accredited in the Direct Access modality (DA) is recognized, the accreditation process will be aligned with the Board's decision. The AC will also approve the standard arrangements for the project cycle as well as the criteria and procedures for accrediting entities. This includes the approval of the accreditation guidelines and the establishment of a robust system to monitor and evaluate the performance of accredited entities. The AC will ensure that the accreditation process is transparent, participatory, and inclusive.

### Experience and Lessons Learned

The GCF has been operating since 2015 and has developed extensive experience in implementing projects. This includes:

- **Country Programming**: Special Climate Change Fund (SCCF) and Least Developed Countries Fund (LDCF) implementation.
- **Project Preparation**: Support to countries to develop projects, including technical assistance and capacity building.
- **Lessons Learned**: Identification of best practices and challenges faced in project implementation.

UNEP has a long-standing relationship with the GCF, having supported the climate in GEF funding activities. This includes the development of the National Adaptation Programme of Action (NAPA) as well as the establishment of the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The experience gained from these initiatives will be leveraged to ensure effective implementation and management of projects under the GCF.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1: PAGE 18

The support of international climate change funds, including the GCF, United Nations projects, ESA funds and UNDP, will be sought to ensure that the project is implemented in a timely manner and that the objectives are achieved.

The proposed project will be implemented over a five-year period from 2025 to 2030 and executed by the Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (MESD). The MESD is headquartered in Accra, Ghana. The MESD has been involved in climate change mitigation and adaptation projects for many years and has extensive experience in project implementation. It has created several unique plans and programs to address climate change issues. The MESD is responsible for the delivery of the project objectives and will get technical support from the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP), Climate Change Division, Ministry of Environment, Science, Technology and Innovation (MESTI) and the Ghana Statistical Service (GSS). The MESD will be the main implementing agency for the project.

As an Executing Entity, the MESD will ensure that the project is implemented at an advanced level and will report UNEP on the NE. To ensure that the project remains on track with national policies, the MESD and UNDP will work closely together to achieve the objectives of the project.

## Project Stakeholders

The MESD will be the main implementing agency for the project. The Ministry of Finance (MoF) and the Public Investment Committee (PIC) will be consulted at regular intervals from the MESD. The MoF is responsible for the financial management of the project, including budgeting, procurement, payments, and auditing. The PIC is responsible for ensuring that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved project proposal.

### Technical Committee

The Technical Committee will be established under the chairmanship of the Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (MESD) to provide technical guidance on the implementation of the project. The committee will include representatives from the MESD, UNDP, MESTI, GSS, and other relevant organizations. The Technical Committee will meet at least twice a year to review progress and provide recommendations for improvement.

### Project Management Unit

The PMU will be established with the MESD and will consist of: i) A Project Coordinator (PC); ii) A Finance Officer (FO); iii) An Environmental Specialist (ES); iv) A Technical Officer (TO). The PC is responsible for overall project management, including coordination with UNDP and other stakeholders. The FO is responsible for financial management, including budgeting, procurement, payments, and auditing. The ES is responsible for environmental management, including monitoring and evaluation of the project's environmental impact. The TO is responsible for technical support, including implementation of the project's technical components.

## Project Implementation

The project will be implemented over a five-year period from 2025 to 2030. The MESD will be the main implementing agency for the project and will work closely with UNDP and other stakeholders to ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved project proposal.

### Project Timeline

The project timeline is as follows:

- **Year 1 (2025)**: Preparation of the project implementation plan, including identification of key activities and milestones.
- **Years 2-4 (2026-2028)**: Implementation of the project activities, including procurement, construction, and monitoring.
- **Year 5 (2029)**: Finalization of the project, including evaluation, reporting, and closure.

### Project Budget

The project budget is as follows:

| Item                  | Cost (USD) |
|-----------------------|------------|
| Preparation          | 100,000    |
| Implementation       | 800,000    |
| Monitoring           | 50,000     |
| Evaluation            | 30,000     |
| Contingency Fund      | 50,000     |
| **Total**             | **1,040,000** |

### B.5 Justification for GCF Funding Requested (1000 words, exclusively per page)
Green finance from GCF can play a vital role in addressing green financing gaps in the region. The proposed project aims to address these gaps by providing financial support to communities and businesses that are implementing climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

The project will focus on providing financial support to small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) that are implementing renewable energy projects. These SMEs often lack access to traditional financing, which limits their ability to invest in green technologies. The proposed project will provide grants and low-interest loans to these SMEs, allowing them to invest in renewable energy projects.

The project will also focus on providing financial support to communities that are implementing climate change adaptation projects. These communities often lack the resources to implement these projects, which can be expensive and time-consuming. The proposed project will provide grants and low-interest loans to these communities, allowing them to implement climate change adaptation projects.

Communities living along the Saltee Salt Marshes of Mauritania are highly vulnerable to the impacts of climate change. The proposed project aims to address these vulnerabilities by providing financial support to communities that are implementing climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

# GREAT LAKES FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 - PAGE 2

The Great Lakes Restoration Initiative (GLRI) is a multi-year federal program to support the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency's (EPA) efforts to protect and restore the Great Lakes. The GLRI was established in 2010 with an initial investment of $475 million, and has since been reauthorized multiple times.

## Objectives

The primary objectives of the GLRI are:

- To support sustainable fishing, agriculture, and economic development in the Great Lakes region.
- To protect and restore ecosystems, including wetlands, forests, and coastal areas.
- To improve water quality and reduce pollution from non-point sources such as agricultural runoff and urban stormwater.

## Funding Allocation

The GLRI has allocated funds to various projects across the Great Lakes region. Some of the key funding areas include:

- **Agricultural Non-Point Source Management**: $180 million
- **Wetland Restoration**: $25 million
- **Great Lakes Fish and Wildlife Restoration**: $30 million

## Implementation Strategy

The implementation strategy for the GLRI includes a combination of federal, state, and local efforts. The EPA works closely with other federal agencies, state governments, and non-profit organizations to achieve the goals of the initiative.

### Key Strategies

1. **Restoration Projects**: Funding for large-scale restoration projects aimed at improving water quality and habitat.
2. **Education and Outreach**: Programs to educate the public about the importance of protecting the Great Lakes.
3. **Partnerships**: Collaborations with local, state, and federal agencies to maximize resources.

### Timeline

The GLRI is a long-term initiative with a focus on sustainable outcomes. The timeline for implementation includes:

- **Short-term (1-2 years)**: Initial planning and project setup.
- **Medium-term (3-5 years)**: Implementation of key projects.
- **Long-term (6+ years)**: Monitoring, evaluation, and sustained improvements.

### Risk Strategy

The risk strategy involves regular monitoring and evaluation to ensure the effectiveness of the GLRI. Regular assessments are conducted to identify potential issues and adjust strategies accordingly.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.1.0 PAGE 3

## B

### Renewability through technical and institutional capacity building

The project will focus on enhancing the capacity of local governments to improve energy efficiency and reduce greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions in Mauritius. This includes providing training, technical assistance, and financial support for the adoption of renewable energy technologies such as solar photovoltaic systems, wind turbines, and energy-efficient appliances.

Equipment provided through the project will also provide respect to GHG, CTCs, comparability, additionality, and sustainability criteria. The equipment will be procured from local suppliers where possible to ensure that the project contributes to local economic development. The project will also promote the use of renewable energy technologies in households and small businesses.

### Social and environmental sustainability

Social and environmental sustainability means that community participation and awareness of the project is essential for its success. The project will focus on raising awareness among local communities, particularly women and youth, about the benefits of renewable energy and how to access it through CTCs. Women's groups and other community-level organizations will be involved in the planning and implementation of the project.

### Financial sustainability

Financial sustainability means that no government and community funds will rely on the continued ODA of project funds. The project will aim to achieve financial sustainability by providing training and technical assistance to local governments and communities to develop their own renewable energy projects. This will include providing grants and low-interest loans to support the development of renewable energy projects.

A global environment facility (GEF) small grant facility will be established at an ongoing mechanism under the Ministry of Environment, Sustainable Development, and Climate Change. The GEF SGP will provide financial support and technical assistance to support the implementation of the project.

To achieve this objective, the project will work with the following government entity to build capacity in local governments, households, and small businesses to implement renewable energy projects. This includes providing training and technical assistance to local governments, households, and small businesses to develop their own renewable energy projects.

At the household level, the financial benefits include through improved 5 climate resilient agriculture and disaster risk reduction measures. The project will also provide training and technical assistance to households to develop their own renewable energy projects.

# C.3 Capacity building and technology development (marker (max. 250 words, approximately 3.5 pages))

## C.3 Outputs: GC training through capacity building activities?

**Yes:** 97 **No:** 3

### Technology transfer

The project will create a conducive environment for climate change and gender responsive food use planning and natural resource management. Monitoring, technology transfer through several key activities will be implemented to ensure the sustainability of the technologies. These include: (i) training of trainers; (ii) demonstration plots; (iii) field days; (iv) exchange visits; and (v) production of extension materials.

### Capacity building interventions

One of the project objectives is to facilitate technology transfer by implementing SAK interventions to restore the degraded land. The capacity building activities will include:

- Establishing 2,123 ha of gender-friendly tree-based systems.
- Using agroforestry practices for soil conservation and carbon sequestration.
- Providing training on sustainable land management.

These interventions will be implemented through a combination of training, demonstration plots, field days, exchange visits, and production of extension materials. The project will also provide training to local communities on sustainable land management practices.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## EXPECTED PERFORMANCE AGAINST INVESTMENT CRITERIA

### Introduction

This section provides an overview of the expected performance against the investment criteria as set out in the GCF's Investment Framework.

#### d) Impacted people (over 500 women, approximately 1 percent)

- The project will directly benefit over 322,000 people living within the two priority communities affected by the large haulage and associated activities.
- This includes direct employment of 495 people in the construction phase and 70 permanent jobs created through the operation of the water parks. The total number of direct beneficiaries represents ~1% of Mauritius's total population and ~10% of the target community.

#### Indirect benefits

These will benefit from the project business indirectly through improved employment opportunities, increased purchasing power, and better access to services and goods. This includes the creation of new businesses and jobs in the area, which is estimated to be around 5-7% of the population in all four target areas. Please refer to Table 23 for a detailed breakdown.

#### Contribution to GCF Results Areas

The project will contribute to the following GCF results areas, namely:

- **AA1** — increased resilience of the most vulnerable people and communities.
- **AA2** — increased health, well-being, food and water security.
- **AA3** — reduced emissions.

### Allocation of the first tranche of people and communities

The project will allocate the first tranche of people and communities to be supported through the following methods:

- Due diligence and verification of the impact on the most vulnerable groups in the target areas, including women and children.
- The project will also ensure that the most vulnerable groups are prioritized by providing specific support for those who need it most.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

Creating an enabling environment through transformational funding and programming

The GCF seeks to promote a paradigm shift in development by supporting countries to achieve their climate change objectives. Accordingly, the proposed project aims to contribute to the achievement of these objectives by providing support for the implementation of climate change mitigation and adaptation projects developed under a country-driven framework. The project will also aim to strengthen the capacity of developing countries to access GCF resources through direct access entities (DAEs) and accredited national designated authorities (NDAs). This will be achieved through the provision of technical assistance, capacity building, and knowledge management under Output 2.3. The proposed project will contribute to the achievement of the GCF's strategic objective of supporting developing countries to achieve a paradigm shift.

## Parenting for raising up successful change adaptation through knowledge sharing

The proposed project aims to support the development of a comprehensive knowledge management system that will enable the GCF to effectively share and disseminate knowledge among its stakeholders. This will include the establishment of a GCF Knowledge Portal, which will serve as a central repository for all GCF-related knowledge products. The portal will be designed to provide easy access to information on climate change adaptation and mitigation, as well as best practices and lessons learned from GCF-funded projects. In addition, the project will support the development of a GCF Academy, which will provide training and capacity building opportunities for stakeholders. This will include the provision of online courses, webinars, and workshops to enhance the skills and knowledge of stakeholders in climate change adaptation and mitigation.

## Fostering an enabling environment for transformational outcomes

The proposed project aims to support the development of a comprehensive knowledge management system that will enable the GCF to effectively share and disseminate knowledge among its stakeholders. This will include the establishment of a GCF Knowledge Portal, which will serve as a central repository for all GCF-related knowledge products. The portal will be designed to provide easy access to information on climate change adaptation and mitigation, as well as best practices and lessons learned from GCF-funded projects. In addition, the project will support the development of a GCF Academy, which will provide training and capacity building opportunities for stakeholders. This will include the provision of online courses, webinars, and workshops to enhance the skills and knowledge of stakeholders in climate change adaptation and mitigation.

## Output 2.3: Sustainable Development Indicators (SDIs) work (approximately 1 page)

The proposed project contributes directly to Output 2.3 of the GCF Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). These goals are aligned with the SDGs adopted by the United Nations General Assembly in 2015, which include SDG 8 - Decent Work and Economic Growth, SDG 9 - Industry, Innovation, and Infrastructure, SDG 11 - Sustainable Cities and Communities, SDG 13 - Climate Action, and SDG 17 - Partnerships for the Goals. The project will support the development of indicators to measure progress towards these goals, as well as provide technical assistance to countries in their efforts to achieve them.

The proposed project will also contribute to the achievement of the GCF's strategic objective of supporting developing countries to achieve a paradigm shift.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1: PAGE 3

revenue compatible with a vision of “Mainstreaming Green Economy Principles” globally designed mainly over the last five years. Massachusetts has been at the forefront of this movement, with the state’s first climate change legislation in 2008 (Global Warming Solutions Act) and subsequent updates in 2016 and 2021. Massachusetts also has a long history of environmental leadership, including the passage of the nation's first bottle bill in 1982.

In 2017, Massachusetts was among the 32 states that sued the Trump administration to challenge its rollback of clean power plant rules. In 2018, the state passed a law requiring all retail electricity sales to be from renewable sources by 2035 and all electricity sales to be carbon-neutral by 2047. Massachusetts also has some of the most stringent energy efficiency standards in the country.

The state's high public health and external costs — estimated at 7% of GDP and 8% of GDP respectively — demonstrate the need for a Green Climate Accord (GCA) scheme to compensate for these costs. The GCA would be modeled on the US Department of Agriculture’s Conservation Reserve Program, which compensates farmers for taking land out of production.

The county's high public health and external costs — estimated at 7% of GDP and 8% of GDP respectively — demonstrate the need for a Green Climate Accord (GCA) scheme to compensate for these costs. The GCA would be modeled on the US Department of Agriculture’s Conservation Reserve Program, which compensates farmers for taking land out of production.

Massachusetts' First Nations Development Corporation (FNDC), updated in 2021, identifies 19 opportunities for achieving carbon neutrality and economic development through renewable energy projects. The FNDC also supports the development of a Green Climate Accord to compensate for the costs associated with these projects.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND PROPOSAL V.3.1: PAGE 5

The project aims to strengthen traditional and school gardens at local schools and national parks in small communities. The project is consistent with the country's National Adaptation Programme of Action (NAPA), adopted in 2007, which encourages a cross-sectoral approach to climate change adaptation.

The project's objectives are aligned with the county NAPA, which aims to enhance food security and livelihoods through sustainable agriculture. The project will support the development of school gardens and national parks by providing training on climate-smart agricultural practices. This will help communities adapt to climate change and improve their resilience to extreme weather events.

The project is complementary to the country's ongoing GCF-supported NAP development process, which aims to enhance the integration of climate change adaptation into national policies and strategies. The project will also support the development of a comprehensive climate change risk management plan for the country.

The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Agriculture in collaboration with local communities and other stakeholders. The project will be carried out over a period of five years, with an initial phase of one year for planning and preparation. The project will be funded through a combination of government and international funding sources.

## Stakeholder engagement

The project will engage with a wide range of stakeholders, including local communities, schools, national parks, and other relevant organizations. The project will also involve the private sector in the development of climate-smart agricultural practices. The project will be implemented in close collaboration with the Ministry of Agriculture and the National Adaptation Programme of Action (NAPA) Secretariat.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3: PAGE 5

The economic analysis estimated the net present value (NPV) of the net benefits (including direct and indirect benefits) from the project to be USD 107 million, which is equivalent to a benefit-cost ratio (BCR) of 2.46. This represents an internal rate of return (IRR) of 18.5% per annum.

Project benefits included in the economic analysis are:
- Direct and indirect employment
- Water security benefits for households and agriculture
- Improved livelihoods from increased agricultural production
- Afforestation/reforestation benefits for reducing greenhouse gas emissions, and improved ecosystem services and resilience to climate change.
- Youth generation opportunities resulting through the good of services and trading households.

The GCF project is estimated to generate 107 million USD in net benefits. This estimate was derived by applying a 5% discount rate to the net present value (NPV) of the net benefits over the lifetime of the project.

## D

This investment GCF grant investment will be supplemented by in-kind co-financing of USD 327 million from the MEMD and regional authorities in the target countries. Co-finance from the MEMD and regional authorities will be used to cover the costs of project preparation, implementation and monitoring. With the support of GCF grant funding, the proposed project will contribute to climate change mitigation through sustainable land and water resource management, contributing to a reduction when the country’s approach to addressing the impacts of climate change.

Beyond the direct co-financing, the proposed project is strategically positioned to leverage long-term financing utilizing the Green Climate Fund (GCF) Readiness Program. The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources Management of the Principality of Monaco (MEMD) with around USD 1 million in co-finance from the MEMD. The project will integrate Climate Risk Management, Programmatic approaches, capacity building, and a climate change adaptation component. The project will also support regional authorities and local governments to enhance their capacities to address climate change impacts through improved land and water resource management. This will contribute to sustainable use of forest and water resources, reducing pressure on the impacts of climate change-induced disasters.

## LOGICAL FRAMEWORK

### E.1 Project/Programme Focus

- Reduced emissions (mitigation)
- Increased resilience (adaptation)

### E.2 GCF Impact Level: Paradigm shift potential (may 600 words, approximately 1-2 pages):

#### Assessment:

| Description | Current state (baseline) | Results |
|-------------|--------------------------|---------|
| **There has been minimal progress in addressing climate change mitigation and adaptation needs in Mozambique.** The country is highly vulnerable to climate change impacts. National environmental management strategies have not been effectively implemented. There are no clear policies or plans for climate change mitigation and adaptation. | The current state is characterized by the lack of a comprehensive approach to address climate change issues. The government has not yet developed a national strategy to mitigate and adapt to climate change. There is limited capacity within the government to implement effective measures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance resilience to climate impacts. | The project aims to achieve a paradigm shift in addressing climate change mitigation and adaptation needs. This will be done through the development of comprehensive policies, strategies, and action plans that are aligned with international best practices. The project will also support capacity building for government officials and other stakeholders to effectively implement these measures. |

#### Results:

- Local communities have been engaged through participatory approaches to identify climate change risks and develop local adaptation strategies.
- The project has supported the development of a national greenhouse gas inventory and emissions reduction targets.
- The project has facilitated the integration of climate change considerations into sectoral policies and plans.

### How the project/programme will contribute:

- **Contributing to national-level policy and planning:** The project will support the development of comprehensive policies, strategies, and action plans that are aligned with international best practices. This will help to ensure that Mozambique is better prepared to address climate change mitigation and adaptation needs.
- **Enhancing capacity:** The project will provide training and technical assistance to government officials and other stakeholders to enhance their capacity to implement effective measures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance resilience to climate impacts.
- **Promoting regional cooperation:** The project will facilitate the exchange of information and best practices with other countries in the region. This will help to promote regional cooperation and collaboration on climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE 38

## Green Climate Fund (GCF)

The GCF was established to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change and its impacts. It aims to promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways.

### Objectives of the GCF

- **Mitigate** greenhouse gas emissions
- **Adapt** to climate change impacts
- **Promote** environmental and social co-benefits

## Funding Mechanism

The GCF operates through accredited entities, which include multilateral development banks, UN agencies, and other public institutions. These entities submit funding proposals for projects that align with the GCF's objectives.

### Accreditation Process

1. **Initial Screening**: The GCF Secretariat reviews proposals to ensure they meet basic criteria.
2. **Due Diligence**: Proposals undergo a thorough review process to assess their technical and financial viability, environmental and social safeguards, and alignment with the GCF's strategic priorities.
3. **Approval**: The GCF Board makes final decisions on funding.

### Project Cycle

1. **Identification**: Potential projects are identified based on national climate change strategies and plans.
2. **Preparation**: Detailed project proposals are developed in consultation with stakeholders.
3. **Submission**: Proposals are submitted to the GCF Secretariat for review.
4. **Evaluation**: The GCF Secretariat evaluates proposals against predefined criteria, including environmental impact, social benefits, and financial sustainability.
5. **Approval**: Projects approved by the GCF Board receive funding.
6. **Implementation**: Funds are disbursed, and projects are executed according to the approved plans.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

The GCF has a robust system for monitoring and evaluating funded projects. This includes:

- Regular progress reports from accredited entities
- Independent evaluations of project outcomes
- Feedback loops to improve future funding decisions

This comprehensive approach ensures that GCF funds are used effectively to support climate action in developing countries.

### E.4. Implementation Arrangements

- **Common trust fund (CTF)**: GCF, private sector, and other donors contribute to the CTF.
- **Project cycle**: Project identification, preparation, appraisal, approval, implementation, and supervision.
- **GCF and RMF**: GCF funds projects through RMF, which manages project implementation and reporting.

### E.5. Monitoring and Evaluation

- **Baseline data collection**: Conducted in 2019 to establish baseline indicators.
- **Monitoring framework**: Regularly tracks progress against targets using MoV.
- **Annual reports**: Submitted to GCF and RMF detailing project implementation, results, and challenges.

### E.6. Lessons Learned

- **Project preparation**: Learning lessons from past projects in Mozambique improved the efficiency of the project cycle.
- **Capacity building**: Training local communities on climate resilience practices enhanced project outcomes.
- **Risk management**: Implementing risk management strategies reduced unexpected delays and costs.

### E.7. Assumptions and Risks

- **Assumptions**:
  - Continued access to funding from GCF and other donors.
  - Successful implementation of projects as planned.
  - No significant changes in climate patterns affecting project areas.

- **Risks**:
  - Funding delays or reductions could impact project timelines.
  - Natural disasters or climate-related events may affect project outcomes.
  - Political instability or security issues could hinder project implementation.

### E.8. Reporting and Approval Processes

- **Annual reports**: Submitted to GCF and RMF detailing progress, challenges, and lessons learned.
- **Project-level monitoring**: Regular updates on individual projects' performance.
- **Approval processes**: Projects must meet GCF and RMF standards for approval and funding.

| Core Indicator | Narrative Description | Expected Results (Quantitative) | Expected Results (Qualitative) | How the project will contribute to expected results | Co-benefits |
|----------------|-----------------------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|-------------|
| **Core Indicator 5**<br>Development of enabling environment for climate change adaptation and mitigation policies and strategies | The project will support the development of a comprehensive national adaptation plan (NAP) process that is aligned with the Paris Agreement. This includes the integration of gender-responsive climate change adaptation into national policies and strategies, as well as the promotion of regional cooperation on climate change adaptation. The project will also support the development of a national greenhouse gas inventory system and the implementation of a monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) system for climate change mitigation activities. | * Substantial increase in the number of countries that have adopted and implemented national adaptation plans (NAPs) consistent with the Paris Agreement.<br>* Significant improvement in the integration of gender-responsive climate change adaptation into national policies and strategies.<br>* Increased regional cooperation on climate change adaptation. | * Enhanced capacity of national governments to develop and implement comprehensive NAPs.<br>* Improved integration of gender-responsive climate change adaptation into national policies and strategies.<br>* Strengthened regional cooperation on climate change adaptation. | The project will contribute to the expected results by providing technical assistance and capacity building to national governments, supporting the development of a comprehensive NAP process that is aligned with the Paris Agreement, and promoting regional cooperation on climate change adaptation. The project will also support the development of a national greenhouse gas inventory system and the implementation of an MRV system for climate change mitigation activities. | Multiple co-benefits include improved governance, increased resilience to climate change, enhanced gender equality, and strengthened regional cooperation. |

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1: PAGE 9

## E. K. Project program activities and deliverables

### Activities

**Activity 1:** Strengthened implementation and spacing of Economic Empowerment Agriculture (EEA) measures across the Tana River Delta County in Kenya.

- **Description:**
  - EEA measures will be implemented to enhance agricultural productivity, food security, and income generation.
  - The project will focus on sustainable land management practices, including soil conservation techniques and water harvesting systems.
  - Capacity building for farmers on modern farming methods and technologies.
  - Establishment of farmer field schools to promote best practices in agriculture.

- **Sub-activities:**
  - EEA measures implemented across the Tana River Delta County.
    - 10% increase in agricultural productivity.
    - 5% increase in household incomes.
  - Soil conservation techniques adopted by farmers.
    - 20% reduction in soil erosion.
    - 15% increase in water retention capacity of soils.

- **Deliverables:**
  - Implementation of EEA measures across the Tana River Delta County.
    - 1,000 hectares of land under sustainable land management practices.
    - 30 farmer field schools established.
    - 2,000 farmers trained on modern farming methods and technologies.

### Notes

- Baseline surveys to be conducted in Year 1 of project implementation as reported in the final annual report.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE 91

## Chapter I.3: Knowledge products and services

### Article I.3.1: Development of knowledge products and services

- Facilitate the integration and management of GCF resources to enhance the development, delivery, and use of climate-related knowledge products and services.
- Support the development of a knowledge platform that provides access to climate-related information, data, and tools for developing countries.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations, including government agencies, academic institutions, and private sector entities.

### Article I.3.2: Dissemination of knowledge products

- Actively promote the systematic dissemination of knowledge products through various channels, including digital platforms, workshops, and conferences.
- Ensure that knowledge products are accessible to a wide range of stakeholders, including policymakers, practitioners, and researchers.
- Foster collaboration with international organizations and regional initiatives to enhance the impact of knowledge products.

### Article I.3.3: Health in general gender inclusion

- Promote health equity by addressing the specific needs of marginalized groups, including women, children, and persons with disabilities.
- Support initiatives that aim to improve access to healthcare services for vulnerable populations.
- Encourage partnerships with local organizations and community groups to enhance the delivery of health services.

### Article I.3.4: Gender equality

- Identify gender-specific barriers and needs in relation to climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts.
- Develop and implement strategies to address gender inequalities and promote equal opportunities for women and men.
- Support initiatives that aim to empower women and girls, and enhance their participation in decision-making processes.

### Article I.3.5: One health approach

- Promote a holistic approach to health that recognizes the interconnections between people, animals, plants, and their shared environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to improve human, animal, and plant health through integrated approaches.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one health approaches.

### Article I.3.6: One planet approach

- Promote a sustainable approach to development that recognizes the need to protect and preserve the natural environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote sustainable consumption and production patterns.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one planet approaches.

## Sub-section 1.3.1: Gender equality

### Article I.3.7: Promotion of gender equality

- Promote gender equality by addressing the specific needs of women and girls in relation to climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts.
- Support initiatives that aim to empower women and girls, and enhance their participation in decision-making processes.
- Encourage partnerships with local organizations and community groups to enhance the delivery of services for women and girls.

### Article I.3.8: Gender-responsive budgeting

- Implement gender-responsive budgeting to ensure that resources are allocated effectively to address the specific needs of women and girls.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote gender equality in budgeting processes.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of gender-responsive budgeting.

### Article I.3.9: Gender mainstreaming

- Mainstream gender into all policies, programs, and projects to ensure that they are gender-responsive and promote gender equality.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote gender mainstreaming in all sectors.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of gender mainstreaming.

## Sub-section 1.3.2: One health approach

### Article I.3.10: Promotion of one health approach

- Promote a holistic approach to health that recognizes the interconnections between people, animals, plants, and their shared environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to improve human, animal, and plant health through integrated approaches.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one health approaches.

### Article I.3.11: One health policy

- Develop and implement a comprehensive one health policy that recognizes the interconnections between people, animals, plants, and their shared environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote integrated approaches to human, animal, and plant health.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one health policies.

### Article I.3.12: One health governance

- Establish a governance structure for one health that recognizes the interconnections between people, animals, plants, and their shared environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote integrated approaches to human, animal, and plant health.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one health governance.

## Sub-section 1.3.3: One planet approach

### Article I.3.13: Promotion of one planet approach

- Promote a sustainable approach to development that recognizes the need to protect and preserve the natural environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote sustainable consumption and production patterns.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one planet approaches.

### Article I.3.14: One planet policy

- Develop and implement a comprehensive one planet policy that recognizes the need to protect and preserve the natural environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote sustainable consumption and production patterns.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one planet policies.

### Article I.3.15: One planet governance

- Establish a governance structure for one planet that recognizes the need to protect and preserve the natural environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote sustainable consumption and production patterns.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one planet governance.

## Sub-section 1.3.4: One health-one planet approach

### Article I.3.16: Promotion of one health-one planet approach

- Promote a holistic approach to development that recognizes the interconnections between people, animals, plants, and their shared environment.
- Support initiatives that aim to promote integrated approaches to human, animal, plant health, and sustainable development.
- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one health-one planet approaches.

### Article I.3.17: One health-one planet policy

- Support initiatives that aim to promote integrated approaches to human, animal, plant health, and sustainable development.

- Encourage partnerships with relevant organizations and stakeholders to enhance the implementation of one health-one planet governance.

## Activity 2.1.1: Estimation 2.1.0

Under this activity, EIA/Aure will monitor and evaluate the effectiveness of the measures taken to reduce greenhouse gas emissions from the project area. This includes the following major tasks:

- **Task 2.1.1.a**: Measure and report on the baseline greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Task 2.1.1.b**: Monitor and report on the changes in greenhouse gas emissions over time.

### Support GCF's Six-Step Gender Mainstreaming Approach

Support GCF's six-step gender mainstreaming approach to ensure that the project benefits women, men, girls, and boys equally. This includes:

- **Step 2.1.1.a**: Identify - 2,023 kg of CO2 equivalent emissions reductions.
- **Step 2.1.1.b**: Integrate - Gender-responsive interventions will be integrated into the project design.
- **Step 2.1.1.c**: Plan - Gender equality objectives and indicators will be included in the project's logic framework.
- **Step 2.1.1.d**: Budget - Allocate resources for gender-related activities.
- **Step 2.1.1.e**: Implement - Ensure that all interventions are gender-responsive.
- **Step 2.1.1.f**: Monitor and Evaluate - Track progress towards gender equality objectives.

### Task 2.1.1.a: Measure Baseline Greenhouse Gas Emissions

This task involves the following sub-tasks:

- **Sub-task 2.1.1.a.i**: Conduct a baseline greenhouse gas emissions inventory.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.a.ii**: Validate the baseline greenhouse gas emissions inventory through independent verification.

### Task 2.1.1.b: Monitor and Report on Greenhouse Gas Emissions

This task involves the following sub-tasks:

- **Sub-task 2.1.1.b.i**: Establish a system for monitoring and reporting greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.b.ii**: Collect data on greenhouse gas emissions regularly.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.b.iii**: Analyze the collected data to track changes in greenhouse gas emissions over time.

### Task 2.1.1.c: Gender Mainstreaming

This task involves the following sub-tasks:

- **Sub-task 2.1.1.c.i**: Conduct a gender analysis to identify gender-related barriers and opportunities.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.c.ii**: Develop a gender-responsive project design that addresses identified barriers and opportunities.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.c.iii**: Integrate gender equality objectives into the project's logic framework.

### Task 2.1.1.d: Budget for Gender-Related Activities

This task involves the following sub-tasks:

- **Sub-task 2.1.1.d.i**: Allocate resources in the project budget for gender-related activities.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.d.ii**: Ensure that the budget is flexible enough to accommodate any additional gender-related needs that may arise during implementation.

### Task 2.1.1.e: Implement Gender-Responsive Interventions

This task involves the following sub-tasks:

- **Sub-task 2.1.1.e.i**: Develop and implement interventions that are responsive to the gender-related needs identified in the gender analysis.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.e.ii**: Monitor the implementation of these interventions to ensure they are effective in addressing gender-related barriers and opportunities.

### Task 2.1.1.f: Monitor and Evaluate Gender Equality Objectives

This task involves the following sub-tasks:

- **Sub-task 2.1.1.f.i**: Develop indicators to track progress towards the project's gender equality objectives.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.f.ii**: Collect data on these indicators regularly.
- **Sub-task 2.1.1.f.iii**: Analyze the collected data to assess progress towards the project's gender equality objectives.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0: PAGE 54

## Activity 2.1: Fast Adoption Program for Renewable Energy Technologies (FAPRET)

Activity 2.1.1 Fast Adoption Program for Renewable Energy Technologies (FAPRET) is a program designed to promote the adoption of renewable energy technologies in rural areas where grid electricity is either unavailable or unreliable. The program aims to provide financial and technical support to local communities, non-governmental organizations, and private sector entities that are implementing renewable energy projects. The program will focus on providing grants and low-interest loans for the procurement of renewable energy equipment, capacity building, and technical assistance.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.1.1.1: Establish a Fast Adoption Program for Renewable Energy Technologies (FAPRET) to promote the adoption of renewable energy technologies in rural areas.
- 2.1.1.2: Develop guidelines, criteria, and procedures for the selection and implementation of renewable energy projects under FAPRET.
- 2.1.1.3: Provide financial and technical support to local communities, non-governmental organizations, and private sector entities implementing renewable energy projects.

## Activity 2.2: Strengthen Community-Based Water User Groups (CBWUGs)

Activity 2.2.1 Establish Community-Based Water User Groups (CBWUGs) will be established in selected watersheds to promote sustainable water management practices. CBWUGs will be responsible for the implementation of water conservation and management activities, including the maintenance of irrigation systems, water harvesting structures, and other water-related infrastructure.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.2.1.1: Establish Community-Based Water User Groups (CBWUGs) in selected watersheds.
- 2.2.1.2: Develop guidelines for the establishment of CBWUGs.
- 2.2.1.3: Provide training and capacity building to CBWUG members on sustainable water management practices.

### Activity 2.2.2: Strengthen Capacity of CBWUGs

Activity 2.2.2 will focus on strengthening the capacity of existing Community-Based Water User Groups (CBWUGs) through training, technical assistance, and the provision of equipment and materials. The program will also support CBWUGs in developing and implementing water management plans.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.2.1.4: Conduct needs assessments to identify capacity gaps within existing CBWUGs.
- 2.2.1.5: Develop training modules and materials on sustainable water management practices.
- 2.2.1.6: Provide technical assistance and equipment to CBWUGs for the implementation of water management activities.

### Activity 2.3: Support Climate Resilience Initiatives

Activity 2.3 will support climate resilience initiatives in vulnerable communities by providing financial and technical support for the implementation of climate adaptation measures. The program will focus on supporting community-based initiatives that aim to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities to the impacts of climate change.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.3.1.1: Conduct vulnerability assessments to identify climate risks in vulnerable communities.
- 2.3.1.2: Develop and implement climate adaptation plans for vulnerable communities.
- 2.3.1.3: Provide financial and technical support to community-based climate resilience initiatives.

### Activity 2.4: Enhance Climate Information Services

Activity 2.4 will enhance the capacity of national meteorological services to provide timely and accurate climate information to vulnerable communities. The program will focus on supporting the development and implementation of climate information services, including the establishment of early warning systems for extreme weather events.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.4.1.1: Conduct assessments to identify gaps in climate information services.
- 2.4.1.2: Develop and implement a strategy to enhance the capacity of national meteorological services.
- 2.4.1.3: Establish early warning systems for extreme weather events.

### Activity 2.5: Promote Climate-Smart Agriculture

Activity 2.5 will promote climate-smart agriculture practices among farmers by providing training, technical assistance, and financial support. The program will focus on supporting the adoption of climate-resilient agricultural practices, including the use of improved seeds, crop diversification, and sustainable farming techniques.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.5.1.1: Conduct assessments to identify climate risks in agriculture.
- 2.5.1.2: Develop training modules on climate-smart agriculture practices.
- 2.5.1.3: Provide technical assistance and financial support to farmers for the adoption of climate-resilient agricultural practices.

### Activity 2.6: Enhance Disaster Risk Reduction (DRR) Capacity

Activity 2.6 will enhance the capacity of communities to prepare for and respond to disasters by providing training, technical assistance, and financial support. The program will focus on supporting community-based disaster risk reduction initiatives, including the development and implementation of early warning systems and contingency plans.

### Outputs/Expected Achievements

- 2.6.1.1: Conduct assessments to identify disaster risks in communities.
- 2.6.1.2: Develop training modules on disaster risk reduction practices.
- 2.6.1.3: Provide technical assistance and financial support to community-based disaster risk reduction initiatives.
